- **Reviewer disposition (FB-201):** Round 1 requested `FB-201-B1` (neutral package boundary) and `B2` (defensive `FieldPatch` immutability); round 2 verified both fixes and raised tracker-only `B3`; round 3 raised tracker-only `B4`. Round 4 APPROVED with `B1`–`B4` resolved and no open blocking finding. `FB-006-NB1` is resolved by moving generated `FirebaseEmulatorConfig` to `com.fluxit.config`, updating platform imports, and deleting stale generated output.
- **What `FB-105` did, precisely (do not re-derive from the ledger prose alone — this is the load-bearing summary):**
  - **`DEC-006`** (recorded 2026-09-18, before delegation) settled `FB-104-NB2`: the gate now times out a hung initial `restoreSession()` after **10 seconds**, falling back to the signed-out auth screen with a non-alarming "could not restore your session" notice plus a retry affordance — never an error screen, never `ResolutionFailed`. Implemented as `SessionGateViewModel.InitialRestorationTimeout = 10.seconds`, a named, retunable companion constant. Reviewer verified the fallback holds even when a cached `Authenticated` session was already reported (timing out is not permission to trust an unvalidated credential). Covered by 6 automated virtual-time tests (the suite never sleeps) — **not exercised manually on a device this round.**
  - **Headline correctness result — the `key(uid)` teardown assumption carried since `FB-104` was FALSE.** Reading `navigation3-runtime`/`lifecycle-viewmodel-navigation3` sources directly showed `rememberViewModelStoreNavEntryDecorator()` defaults to the root `ViewModelStoreOwner`, and per-entry stores are keyed by `NavEntry.contentKey` — `DashboardRoute` is a singleton `data object`, so that key never changes across users, and disposing a composition (all `key()` does) pops nothing from the back stack. Two real leaks resulted: (1) user B was handed user A's `DashboardViewModel` instance; (2) the auth form's email field carried A's text into B's sign-in. Both reproduced on-device before the fix.
  - **Fix:** a new teardown boundary, `SessionScopedViewModelStores.kt` (125 lines, new), a root `ViewModel` handing out a fresh `ViewModelStore` per `SessionScope` (`SignedOut` | `User(uid)`), provided as `LocalViewModelStoreOwner` by the gate, clearing the outgoing store on every scope change. Signed-out is a first-class scope, so the auth form gets the same boundary (the developer explicitly rejected an alternative of making `AuthViewModel` self-reset by observing `session`, since that adds a second app-lifetime SDK-listener collection). `key(uid)` is retained but now understood correctly — it only resets the back stack, not any `ViewModelStore`.
  - **`DEC-003a` honored as scoped:** `signOut()` stays Auth-only; no Firestore/Storage SDK surface added (both adapters' stale "FB-105's job" KDoc corrected to name the real deferral — Phase 2 Firestore / Phase 3 Storage — comment-only diffs, reviewer-confirmed zero functional change).
  - **`FB-104-NB1` (the `yield()` residual race) narrowed, not closed:** restoration now awaits a resolved `latestSession` bounded by the same 10s `DEC-006` budget. A provable fix still needs an `AuthRepository.restoreSession()` contract change, deliberately out of scope again (awaiting a *new* emission would stall every ordinary cold start for the full 10s, since both adapters' `distinctUntilChanged` means a confirming restore emits nothing). Stays genuinely `OPEN`.
- **What `FB-106` must state explicitly — `FB-105-NB1`, the single most important carry-forward from this round.** Phase 1's exit criterion text ("handles session changes without leaking data between users") is **literally broader** than what `FB-105` could achieve. After an A->B switch, B still sees A's seeded Room lists: the pre-existing Room store is one undifferentiated local database with **no per-uid partition at all** (reviewer confirmed by direct reading — `ListRepository`/`ItemRepository`/`DebugSeeder` carry no `uid` parameter anywhere; Room predates the auth system entirely). Partitioning Room inside Phase 1 would be scope creep and throwaway work (Room is slated for deletion in Phase 7). **Ruling: not blocking for `FB-105`.** But `FB-106` **must record explicitly** that the "no leaking data between users" criterion is satisfied only for **auth-derived in-memory state and listeners** (the `SessionScopedViewModelStores` fix, the app-scoped auth listener disposal), with full data isolation deferred to **Phase 2** (`FB-201`+, Firestore `users/{uid}` scoping) and **Phase 7** (Room removal) — not silently assumed complete.

The sweep's "referenced" set must enumerate item documents **regardless of tombstone flag** — a soft-deleted item's `photoRef` counts as still referenced for its full 30-day tombstone window. Distinct from, and additional to, the re-check-eligibility-at-delete-time requirement. The sweep and the tombstone purge share one retention constant that must not drift apart.

Per `PLAN-008`, the iOS Swift bridge must expose a field-mask/changed-keys surface to make this expressible — `FB-203`/`FB-205` must design that up front. A same-field conflict silently loses the earlier value; surfacing that in the UI is deliberately **not** required of `FB-401`/`FB-404`.

Zero `.entitlements` files exist in `iosApp/iosApp/` and none should be added.
  - **iOS facts from `FB-103`, worth not rediscovering the hard way:** ATS does **not** apply to loopback, so `http://127.0.0.1:9099` reached the Auth emulator with **no** `Info.plist` change needed. A **LAN** emulator host (not `127.0.0.1`) would likely need `NSAllowsLocalNetworking`, Debug-scoped — out of scope today. A Kotlin `suspend` function cannot be implemented from Swift, hence the bridge uses completion handlers converted back via `suspendCancellableCoroutine` on the Kotlin side. Kotlin's `(NSError?) -> Unit` parameter type imports into Swift as `((any Error)?) -> Void` — expect this exact signature shape for any future Kotlin/Swift completion-handler bridge. `xcrun simctl launch` on an **already-running** app does **not** re-run `AppDelegate` — the app must be terminated between launches for any `AppDelegate`-driven state to actually re-execute.
  - **Open `FB-000` item, still not moved:** Koin starts in `MainViewController.kt:11-17` (shared `commonMain`/Compose entry point, unchanged this pass — `startKoin { modules(platformModule(), appModule) }` guarded by a `koinStarted` flag), while `FirebaseApp.configure()` runs earlier in `AppDelegate.didFinishLaunchingWithOptions` on iOS. ## Status values

`NOT_STARTED`, `READY`, `IN_PROGRESS`, `IN_REVIEW`, `CHANGES_REQUESTED`, `VERIFIED`, `DONE`, `BLOCKED_USER`, `BLOCKED_TECHNICAL`, `SKIPPED`.

Manual actions use `NOT_STARTED`, `REQUESTED`, `PASS`, `WAIVED`, or `FAILED`. Only `PASS` and a user-approved `WAIVED` satisfy a manual gate. `MAN-*` IDs are linked evidence records, not task dependencies.

Override proven non-vacuous: `-showBuildSettings` with the placeholder `TEAM_ID` set shows `DEVELOPMENT_TEAM` taking that value while `PRODUCT_BUNDLE_IDENTIFIER` stays `com.fluxit.FluxIt`. No team id is recorded in any tracked file (verified by this handoff's own repo-wide search). Not covered at the time this row was written: physical-device signing and embed-and-sign (`MAN-003`/`FB-011`). **Note added after `FB-011`/`DEC-004`:** `MAN-003` is now permanently `WAIVED` — physical-device signing and embed-and-sign are accepted as permanently out of scope for this project, not an open item. |

### P1 — Authentication and session boundary

| FB-101 | DONE | Developer/implementation | Add Firebase-neutral AuthRepository, session/error states, fakes, and shared unit tests. | FB-009 | Common tests cover loading, signed-out, authenticated, error, and restoration transitions. | 2026-09-18. **Reviewer `APPROVED` round 1, no blocking findings.** Deliverable is 4 new files, **zero existing files modified**: `composeApp/src/commonMain/kotlin/com/fluxit/domain/auth/AuthModels.kt` (`AuthUser`, `AuthSession`, `AuthError`, `AuthResult` + `isResolved`/`uidOrNull`/`isSuccess`/`errorOrNull`), `.../domain/auth/AuthRepository.kt` (the interface), `composeApp/src/commonTest/kotlin/com/fluxit/FakeAuthRepository.kt`, `.../commonTest/kotlin/com/fluxit/AuthRepositoryTests.kt` (23 tests in 3 classes). **Surface:** `session: Flow<AuthSession>`; `suspend` `restoreSession`/`signUp`/`signIn`/`sendPasswordResetEmail`/`signOut` returning `AuthResult`. `AuthSession` = `Unresolved` | `SignedOut` | `Authenticated(user)` | `ResolutionFailed(error)` — `Unresolved` vs `SignedOut` are **distinct sealed cases**, which is precisely the distinction `FB-104`'s root gate needs (`uidOrNull` returns null for every non-authenticated state, so no uid can be read before resolution). Interactive-operation failures return `AuthResult.Failure` and leave session state unchanged, so a wrong password does not masquerade as a broken session. `AuthError` = `InvalidCredentials`/`InvalidEmail`/`EmailAlreadyInUse`/`WeakPassword`/`UserNotFound`/`UserDisabled`/`TooManyRequests`/`NetworkUnavailable`/`SessionExpired`/`Unknown`. **Constraints verified by the reviewer, not merely claimed:** Firebase-neutral (`grep -rni 'firebase|com.google'` over all four files -> 0 matches; `git diff` over `gradle/`, `composeApp/build.gradle.kts`, `build.gradle.kts`, `settings.gradle.kts` -> empty, no new dependency); `DEC-001` email+password only, no provider argument; `PLAN-008` iOS-adapter shape sound (all `suspend` over immutable sealed types, one `Flow` of immutable values, no generics or platform types crossing the boundary); `DEC-003a` not contradicted (`signOut` KDoc documents cache teardown explicitly); typed taxonomy with no raw SDK code/string in the commonMain public surface; no regression (Room, ViewModels, navigation, DI, Gradle, Rules, iOS sources all byte-unchanged — only a forward-looking doc comment at `AndroidFirebaseInitializer.kt:19` even mentions these types). **Evidence (`PLAN-004` satisfied — forced, and XML timestamps read from disk by BOTH developer and reviewer independently):** developer ran `./gradlew :composeApp:testDebugUnitTest :composeApp:testReleaseUnitTest :composeApp:iosSimulatorArm64Test --rerun-tasks` at 14:20:35Z-14:20:50Z -> `BUILD SUCCESSFUL`, 73 actionable / **73 executed, 0 UP-TO-DATE**; reviewer **independently re-ran the same command** at 14:22:26Z -> `BUILD SUCCESSFUL`, 73/73 executed, and read all **18 JUnit XML files** directly, confirming XML `timestamp` attributes (Android `14:22:33Z`, iOS `14:22:42Z`) against the reviewer's own `date -u` (`14:22:46Z`). Both agree: **34 tests / 0 skipped / 0 failures / 0 errors in each of the three suites**, split 11 pre-existing (`CreateListViewModelTest` 4, `DashboardViewModelTest` 3, `ListDetailViewModelTest` 4 — unchanged, still green, no regression) + 23 new (`AuthRepositoryFlowsTest` 13, `AuthRepositoryRestorationTest` 6, `AuthSessionStateTest` 4). `./gradlew :composeApp:assembleDebug :composeApp:assembleRelease --rerun-tasks` -> `BUILD SUCCESSFUL`, 96 actionable / **96 executed**, run by developer (14:20:58Z) and reproduced by reviewer (14:22:53Z). **Acceptance criterion met** — all five required transition categories carry real assertions: loading/unresolved (`sessionStartsUnresolvedBeforeRestoration`, `unresolvedIsNotResolvedAndCarriesNoUid`), signed-out (`restoreWithoutPersistedUserResolvesSignedOut`, `signedOutIsResolvedAndDistinctFromUnresolved`, `signOutResolvesSignedOutAndDropsTheUid`), authenticated-with-uid (`restoreWithPersistedUserResolvesAuthenticatedWithUid`, `signUpAuthenticatesTheNewAccount`, `signInWithCorrectCredentialsAuthenticates`, `signingInAsASecondUserReplacesTheUid`), error taxonomy (six distinct `AuthError` cases each asserted), restoration (`restoreFailureIsResolutionFailedAndRetryStillSucceeds`, `restorationTransitionSequenceIsObservable` asserting the exact emitted sequence `Unresolved -> ResolutionFailed -> Authenticated -> SignedOut`), plus `DEC-001` recovery (`passwordRecoveryIsSentForAKnownAccount`, `passwordRecoveryForUnknownAccountReportsUserNotFound`). **Reviewer rulings on three deliberate judgement calls:** fake in `commonTest` not `commonMain` — **correct as shipped**, matches the pre-existing `FakeRepositories.kt` convention and breaks no preview (reviewer verified no `commonMain` `@Preview` references any fake); payload-free `AuthError.Unknown` — **correct as shipped**, a defensible privacy/neutrality tradeoff documented in the contract; no DI wiring — **correct to defer**, since binding an interface with no implementation would fail Koin resolution at runtime, and `FB-102`/`FB-103` bind from `platformModule()`. Recorded as `FB-101-NB1`/`FB-101-NB2`; the DI deferral produced no finding at all. **HONEST BOUNDS — everything here is proven only against an in-memory fake. No real Firebase Auth provider, emulator, or SDK was contacted; no adapter exists yet, so real Android/iOS parity is not yet assessable.** The `PLAN-008` iOS-bridge-friendliness claim is a **design argument, not executed proof** — `FB-103` is its first real test. The error taxonomy's completeness against real SDK codes is unverified until `FB-102`/`FB-103` map them; expect `Unknown` to absorb cases initially (extensibility risk, not correctness). | FB-102 | DONE | Developer/implementation | Implement Android official Auth adapter. | FB-101 | Android integration checks for sign-up/in/recovery/sign-out/restoration. | 2026-09-18. **Reviewer `APPROVED` round 1, no blocking findings**, all evidence independently re-run. **Added:** `composeApp/src/androidMain/kotlin/com/fluxit/firebase/auth/AndroidAuthRepository.kt` (the adapter), `FirebaseAuthGateway.kt` (internal `FirebaseSdkAuthGateway` seam + `AuthStateRegistration` + a `Task`->`suspend` bridge, no new coroutines-play-services dep), `FirebaseAuthErrorMapper.kt` (`AuthDiagnostics`/`LogcatAuthDiagnostics`, pure `authErrorForCode`, `mapAuthFailure`); `composeApp/src/androidUnitTest/.../{AndroidAuthRepositoryTest(17),RecordingAuthGateway,FirebaseAuthErrorMapperTest(4)}`; `composeApp/src/androidInstrumentedTest/.../{FirebaseAuthEmulatorIntegrationTest(13),AndroidAuthDiInstrumentedTest(2)}`; `composeApp/src/debug/AndroidManifest.xml` + `src/debug/res/xml/network_security_config.xml`. **Modified (3 tracked, +23):** `di/PlatformModule.android.kt` (+9, `single<AuthRepository> { AndroidAuthRepository() }`), `composeApp/build.gradle.kts` (+10, `androidInstrumentedTest` deps + `AndroidJUnitRunner`), `gradle/libs.versions.toml` (+4). **No new production dependency** — `firebase-auth` was already an `androidMain` dep from `FB-006`. **FB-101 contract byte-unchanged**, independently re-verified (`git diff HEAD -- commonMain/.../domain/auth/ commonTest/` -> empty). **Design:** `session` is a `callbackFlow` that sends current state first (deterministically `Unresolved` until resolved — `Unresolved`/`SignedOut` stay distinct for `FB-104`'s gate), registers the SDK `AuthStateListener`, relays a private `MutableStateFlow`, `awaitClose { registration.remove(); relay.cancel() }`, `.distinctUntilChanged()`. State lives in the StateFlow, not the flow, so operations with no collector still leave correct state and `restoreSession()` can publish `ResolutionFailed` (a state the SDK listener can never report). `restoreSession()` calls `reload()` against the server for a persisted user, so a revoked/disabled/deleted credential does not pass silently. **`FB-101-NB2` discharged and reviewer-verified at `FirebaseAuthErrorMapper.kt:93-108`:** `mapAuthFailure` calls `diagnostics.onSdkFailure(...)` **before** returning `mapped ?: Unknown`, on every call site; unmapped failures log at `Log.e` with the full throwable and an explicit UNMAPPED marker. Asserted non-trivially by `anUnmappedSdkFailureIsLoggedBeforeItBecomesUnknown` (`assertSame(original, logged.throwable)`). This logging proved its own worth mid-task: the first instrumented run failed with an otherwise-opaque `Unknown`, and the log line was the only thing identifying the cause (blocked cleartext to `10.0.2.2`). **`FB-101-NB3` discharged — reviewer judged this the strongest part of the deliverable and explicitly non-vacuous.** Unit: `aReleasedListenerStopsReceivingUpdatesAndDoesNotResurrectTheCollector` asserts `removeCount==1` + `liveListeners.isEmpty()` **and then** emits a further auth-state change and asserts the emission count did not grow (proves the listener is functionally dead, not just that a counter moved); `cancellingTheCollectorRemovesTheUnderlyingAuthStateListener` (add=1/remove=1), `eachCollectorOwnsItsOwnListenerAndReleasesIt` (2/2). Instrumented against the **real SDK + real emulator**: `cancellingTheCollectorReleasesTheRealSdkAuthStateListener` cancels the collector, performs a genuine `signUp` state change, asserts emissions unchanged. Residual bound: only that `FirebaseAuth.removeAuthStateListener` honors its own internal contract; the observable behavior is proven on a real device. **Error taxonomy:** full code->`AuthError` mapping table (see `FirebaseAuthErrorMapper.kt`), fallback by exception type, else `Unknown`. **5 of 10 cases empirically confirmed against real-SDK-emitted codes** captured from logcat (`ERROR_INVALID_EMAIL`->InvalidEmail, `ERROR_EMAIL_ALREADY_IN_USE`->EmailAlreadyInUse, `ERROR_WEAK_PASSWORD`->WeakPassword, `ERROR_USER_NOT_FOUND`->UserNotFound, `ERROR_WRONG_PASSWORD`->InvalidCredentials). `UserDisabled`/`TooManyRequests`/`NetworkUnavailable`/`SessionExpired`/`Unknown` rest on the docs-derived table plus the pure unit test — **not** empirically observed. **Evidence (`PLAN-004` satisfied; reviewer independently re-ran everything, not re-quoting):** `./gradlew :composeApp:testDebugUnitTest :composeApp:testReleaseUnitTest :composeApp:iosSimulatorArm64Test :composeApp:assembleDebug :composeApp:assembleRelease --rerun-tasks` -> `BUILD SUCCESSFUL`, **122 actionable / 122 executed, 0 UP-TO-DATE** (developer 14:40:13Z-14:40:33Z; reviewer re-run, 18 on-disk JUnit XMLs read directly, `timestamp` attrs 14:43:28Z-14:43:39Z bracketed by the reviewer's own `date -u` at 14:43:15Z/14:43:45Z). Android debug and release each **55 tests / 0 skipped / 0 failures / 0 errors** = 34 pre-existing (all still green, **no regression**) + 21 new; **iOS stays exactly 34/0/0/0** (new tests are Android-only, `FB-101` baseline intact on both). `./gradlew :composeApp:connectedDebugAndroidTest` -> `BUILD SUCCESSFUL`, **15/15 instrumented tests, 0 failures**, on AVD `fluxit_fb008_smoke` (API 36, pre-existing and already booted; **no AVD created or deleted**, `Pixel_10` untouched), against a **real local Firebase Auth emulator** (`--only auth --project demo-fluxit`); reviewer re-ran it (XML 14:44:10Z, bracketed by own wall clock) after confirming the emulator live (`curl 127.0.0.1:9099` -> 200) and the AVD identity (`adb emu avd name`). **Acceptance criterion judged genuinely met at emulator fidelity** by the reviewer: all five required flows (sign-up, sign-in, recovery, sign-out, restoration) carry real instrumented assertions driving the production adapter through the real SDK. Restoration is covered by `aFreshRepositoryOverThePersistedCredentialRestoresTheSameUid` and `restoringWithNoPersistedCredentialResolvesSignedOutNotUnresolved`. **Security — debug cleartext config, reviewer-verified empirically, not waved through:** `network_security_config.xml` permits cleartext for exactly `10.0.2.2`/`127.0.0.1`/`localhost`, all `includeSubdomains="false"`, debug variant only. Reviewer built both merged manifests and grepped them: `android:networkSecurityConfig` present in the **debug** merged manifest, **absent from release**; the XML is packaged only under `packaged_res/debug/`, never `release/`. **New finding with Phase 2 impact:** this is *why* `FB-006`'s emulator code path had never run. Also empirically substantiated: `src/androidDebug/AndroidManifest.xml` is silently **NOT** merged in this KMP/AGP setup; `src/debug/AndroidManifest.xml` is — any Phase 2 Firestore/Storage emulator work needs this same overlay at this same path. **LIVE-PROJECT CONTACT — precise scope, corrected by the reviewer (`FB-102-NB1`); the developer's blanket claim was imprecise and is NOT recorded here:** no auth *operation* (sign-up/sign-in/reset/sign-out/restore) touched the default live-project `FirebaseApp`; all operational tests ran against a dedicated secondary `demo-fluxit` app pointed at the emulator with a fake key. **However**, instrumented runs do initialize the default `FirebaseApp` from the live `google-services.json` via the pre-existing `FluxItApplication.onCreate`, and `AndroidAuthDiInstrumentedTest:29-42` resolves `AuthRepository` from the real Koin graph and calls `session.first()`, which constructs `FirebaseAuth.getInstance()` (the **default**, live-configured instance) and attaches a real `AuthStateListener` to it. That call is **local-only — no network round-trip, no live user read or written, nothing reached the Console** — but it is not accurate to say nothing touched the default app. No test account was created in the live project. **No regression / isolation:** `git diff HEAD --stat` over `iosMain`, `commonMain/data`, `commonMain/ui`, `commonMain/di` empty; Rules and `firebase/` untouched; both mobile config files still gitignored; only credential-shaped literals are throwaway emulator-only fixtures. `AuthRepository` is bound **only** in the Android `platformModule()`; `appModule` has no consumer yet (`FB-104` adds the first), so the iOS graph is unchanged and still resolves (iOS 34/34 green), and the Koin `single` is lazy so the shipping app never instantiates it today. **Reviewer rulings:** the `FirebaseAuthGateway` seam is **justified** (internal, no Firebase types in signature, and is what makes the NB3 unit-level proof possible at all); the debug cleartext config is **sound and tightly scoped**; the `DEC-003a` boundary (`signOut()` = Auth only, Firestore/Storage `terminate()`/`clearPersistence()` deferred) is **correct as scoped, not an under-delivery** — neither SDK is in any data path yet and the `FB-105` ledger row explicitly owns that work; rejecting `testOptions.unitTests.isReturnDefaultValues = true` in favor of instrumented SDK-exception coverage is a **sound tradeoff, honestly disclosed** (it would have silently disarmed that guard for every unit test in the module). **HONEST BOUNDS.** Rate limiting, disabled accounts, token expiry/revocation, real recovery-email delivery, and email-enumeration-protection variants were **NOT** exercised anywhere — those five taxonomy cases rest on documentation plus a pure-function unit test, not observed SDK output. Proving them live would require creating and disabling real accounts in the development project, which is a **user decision the orchestrator has not requested**. No release-configuration runtime test (release was built, not run) — same bound as Phase 0. The integration suite is **not self-provisioning**: it needs a manually started Auth emulator and fails with a network error if it is down; nothing enforces this in CI. Reviewer did not vulnerability-scan the new `androidx.test` test deps, and did not formally prove instrumented-test isolation against stray persisted credentials from prior `FB-008` runs on the same long-lived AVD (per-test UUID emails plus `signOut()` in `@Before`/`@After` mitigate but do not prove it). |

Completion handlers are used instead of `suspend` (a Kotlin `suspend` fn cannot be implemented from Swift) and converted back via `suspendCancellableCoroutine`; `awaitCompletion` guards a double-invoking Swift impl via `continuation.isActive`, genuinely tested by `aBridgeThatCallsBackTwiceDoesNotBreakTheAdapter`. Bridge registered in `FirebaseBootstrap.start()` immediately after `FirebaseApp.configure()` inside `didFinishLaunchingWithOptions` — strictly before `ContentView` starts Koin — and resolved **lazily per call** via `IosAuthBridgeRegistry`, so an early-constructed Koin `single` cannot fail. Added a local generic `Task<T>.awaitTaskResult()` (FB-202's helper is `Task<Void>`-only, untouched). **Counter design:** every counter-affecting mutation (`setCompleted`, `softDeleteItem`, `restoreItem`, `deleteItem`) runs inside `firestore.runTransaction`, reads the item's live `isCompleted`/`deletedAt` first, and only writes a counter delta when it's actually non-zero (idempotent by construction — a repeated or concurrent call reads the already-updated state and computes zero delta); counter scope matches Room's DAO ground truth exactly (`totalItems`=active-only, `completedItems`=active-and-completed-only, read from `Daos.kt:14-15`). `addItem` is an unconditional `totalItems += 1` via one atomic `WriteBatch` (`.set()` new doc + `.update()` counter), justified by the same `DEC-003d-1` creation-only exemption as `createList`. Counter fields are never routed through `FieldPatch` (its constructor `require`s block them) — raw `FieldValue.increment(delta)` only. **Deliberate missing-item asymmetry, flagged for reviewer sign-off:** the four counter-affecting mutations silently no-op if the item doc doesn't exist (mirrors Room's no-match-is-a-no-op `UPDATE ... WHERE` semantics, makes retries after a concurrent hard delete safe); `updateItem`/`setPhotoPath` keep FB-202's existing bare-`.update()`-throws-`NOT_FOUND` behavior. **Chunk strategy for `clearCompleted`:** query-driven loop, `.limit(chunkSize)` (constructor param, default 400, `require`s `chunkSize + 1 <= 500`), one `WriteBatch` per page (item tombstones + trailing counter decrement), loop ends on an empty page; a `MAX_CLEAR_COMPLETED_ITERATIONS = 100_000` guard prevents runaway loops. Evidence: 6 pure JVM boundary tests on the extracted `requireValidClearCompletedChunkSize` function, plus one real-emulator test forcing 3 genuine sequential `WriteBatch.commit()`s (`chunkSize=5`, 14 real items, 5+5+4) that also proves idempotent-retry-on-empty-requery; developer states no test proves the literal 500-document boundary against a live emulator (impractical per the task's own allowance) — flagged as a bounded evidence gap, not silently claimed as full coverage. **Reported commands/results (developer-supplied, not yet reviewer-reproduced):** `testDebugUnitTest`/`testReleaseUnitTest --rerun-tasks` -> 119/119 both (113 FB-202 baseline + 6 new), on-disk JUnit XML mtimes checked; confirmed no iOS/list-repository/Koin DI/Room/Rules/mobile-config files touched; no commit made. **Other developer-flagged residual risks for reviewer:** `clearCompleted` is per-chunk-atomic not whole-operation-atomic (an item concurrently un-completed/restored mid-sweep could still be counted in that chunk); no floor/clamp on `FieldValue.increment` (a pre-existing-drift scenario could drive a counter negative, no reconciliation job exists yet, presumably Phase 6+); "retry" tests are caller-level (repeated method calls), not SDK-transport-level; cross-user-denial and offline coverage are single-method-representative (`addItem` only), matching FB-202's own precedent. (3) `SUPPORTED_TYPES={JPEG,PNG,WebP}` matching what the system pickers actually return, GIF/BMP recognized-but-rejected, HEIC not decoded (pickers already re-encode it); (4) type detection is magic-byte sniffing, never trusting caller MIME/extension; (5) new `PhotoRejected` sealed type, not Firebase/generic, for future `FB-306` UI error-state matching; (6) resize is short-circuited — an already-compliant image uploads byte-for-byte, resize lambda never called, covered by a dedicated test; (7) `photoPreparer` wired as an injected lambda with a real default (not a new DI interface) specifically so `commonTest` never invokes the real platform decoder (Android plain JUnit has no Robolectric; a real `BitmapFactory` call throws "not mocked" in unit tests) while production gets the real decoder via the default parameter with zero Koin changes; (8) no ViewModel-level test for `photoPreparer` throwing `PhotoRejected` through `viewModelScope.launch` — deliberately mirrors `FB-302-NB3`'s same already-accepted gap, tested instead by calling the pure function directly. **Disclosed gap:** no runtime test for the real Android decode/resize actual (no Robolectric in this project, and adding it was judged out of scope/an undisclosed new test dependency) — coverage there is compile-only (`compileDebugKotlinAndroid` BUILD SUCCESSFUL) plus pure fake-injected decision-logic tests; New `composeApp/src/androidInstrumentedTest/kotlin/com/fluxit/data/PhotoStorageEmulatorIntegrationTest.kt` (322 lines, 6 tests, real/non-mocked against the Storage+Auth emulator loading the real deployed `storage.rules`): owner upload/load/delete round-trip at the exact `photoRef` shape; Leg 5 (background/foreground) `PASS` on Android (real UI, `KEYCODE_HOME`/`KEYCODE_APP_SWITCH`, no crash, no re-fetch stall); on iOS, simulator-fidelity-only per `DEC-004`/`FB-011-NB1` (disclosed, accepted bound) and also blocked by the same crash from an authenticated run. **Headline finding — a new, more severe defect than `FB-402-NB1`, live-reproduced, not fixed (out of this task's explicit scope):** no listener-error `.catch` exists around `DashboardViewModel`'s repository-observation flow or `observeListSummariesSnapshot()` on either platform, so a terminal listener error (e.g. a real `PERMISSION_DENIED` once the backing auth token is invalidated — sign-out, a revoked/expired token, or a stale persisted session found invalid on cold launch) propagates uncaught through `viewModelScope` and **crashes the app process** — not a spinner, not an `ApplicationError`, a real `FATAL EXCEPTION`. **iOS: reproduced 4 times live with unmodified production code**, including once on a plain cold launch with no explicit sign-out (a stale persisted session resolved to `SignedOut` while the listener was live); the crash-on-launch condition persisted across simulator uninstall+reinstall (Keychain-persisted Auth tokens outlive app removal) and only cleared after a full `simctl erase`. **Android: same mechanism, reproduced once via a test-harness artifact** (two `DashboardViewModel` instances sharing one `FirebaseApp` via a leaked `WhileSubscribed(5_000)` listener), fixed in the test file itself (`ViewModelStore.clear()` in `finally`) — but the real production sign-out flow via real UI did **not** crash in the developer's live attempt, so Android is disclosed as **not provably safe**, only not-yet-triggered by the one flow tried. Developer correction 2026-09-29: functions/cascade.js now claims an expired list in one Firestore transaction that re-reads deletedAt, creates a durable users/{uid}/listCleanupJobs/{listId} job, and deletes the parent. The mobile clients restore with field-scoped update/updateData: a restore committed before claim preserves the complete list, while a restore attempted after claim fails NOT_FOUND; therefore no accepted restore can coexist with child/photo deletion. Every run first discovers and resumes claimed jobs even though their parent is absent. It then pages up to 100 children per transaction, atomically deletes each item and journals its exact owner/item photoRef under the private job; a Storage failure or process interruption leaves the job for retry. Photos retain the shared 30-day age and fresh-reference checks plus generation-conditional deletion; a job is removed only when items and photo journals are empty. Firestore Rules retain owner-only access and deny client reads/writes to the unmatched job and photo-journal paths. Narrow list-create/update and item-write guards now require no active job and an existing list parent after the batch, respectively; direct client hard list deletion is denied so only Admin cleanup can remove a parent; Rules emulator tests prove delayed item writes, delete-parent-plus-create-child batches, and claimed-list re-creation are denied, while ordinary owner item writes and atomic item+counter batches still pass. FB-507 must deploy these Rules and the indexes before enabling backend cleanup. firestore.indexes.json declares the claimedAt collection-group index used to resume jobs. | FB-003-B1 | `FB-003` review, 2026-09-17 | **RESOLVED 2026-09-17** by `DEC-003e-2` (grace period raised 24h -> 30 days, aligning with `DEC-003b`). | `DEC-003e`'s 24h grace period does not cover the app's own offline-write model. Phase 3's mandated safe-replace order is upload bytes to Storage first, then write the referencing Firestore document. If a client completes the upload and then loses connectivity before the second write lands (or that write sits in Firestore's offline queue), the object is unreferenced for longer than 24h and the sweep deletes it. When the client finally reconnects, the queued write lands pointing at a `photoRef` that no longer exists — a broken reference with no cleanup path back. The record's re-check-at-delete-time mitigation does **not** help: at delete time the referencing document still does not exist server-side, so there is nothing to re-check against. Precedent cited by the reviewer: `DEC-003b` chose 30-day tombstone retention *specifically* to "tolerate delayed clients", and the plan names delayed clients as the reason to size retention longer — so 24h is inconsistent with how this codebase already reasons about offline durability. This changes the correct boundary **value**, so it is a policy gap, not an `FB-502`/`FB-503` testing detail. **Returned to the user by the orchestrator as a re-opened sub-decision (`DEC-003e-2`).** | User (re-decision) |

This **narrows but does not provably close** the window: `AuthRepository.restoreSession()` returns `Unit`, so its outcome is observable only through the `session` flow, and a cross-thread/cross-dispatcher delivery could in principle land after the `yield()`. Reviewer agreed with the developer's own candid self-assessment (stated in the `SessionGateViewModel.kt` KDoc, not hidden) and ruled it non-blocking: both real adapters publish to their internal `MutableStateFlow` synchronously on the same coroutine immediately before returning, so there is **no actual cross-thread hop in the code that exists today** and the risk is speculative against a future adapter change; the worst case is self-correcting (the next emission fixes the gate) and never publishes `Ready` on an unvalidated credential, which is what the acceptance criterion protects. A proper fix requires changing `AuthRepository.restoreSession()` to return its outcome — an **`FB-101` contract change**, deliberately out of `FB-104`'s scope. Must not be reopened as a gate on `FB-104`. **`FB-105` disposition (2026-09-18, reviewer round 1):** narrowed, not closed. No gate-level timeout exists. Neither adapter hung in any run, and both rely on their SDK's own network timeouts. Reviewer ruled deferral acceptable because choosing a timeout duration and a fallback UX is a **product decision, not a correctness fix** — but it is a genuine behavioral trade-off that `FB-105` must pick up deliberately, not inherit silently. If the fallback UX needs deciding, that is a user decision the orchestrator must request. **Resolution (2026-09-18):** the orchestrator obtained the user decision `DEC-006` (10s timeout, fallback to signed-out + non-alarming notice + retry, never an error screen) before delegating `FB-105`; the developer implemented it as `SessionGateViewModel.InitialRestorationTimeout = 10.seconds`, reviewer-verified via `publishGateState` precedence and a passing test proving the fallback holds even when a cached `Authenticated` session was already reported. So this is not A's data leaking to B in the sense the plan's `users/{uid}/...` Firestore model cares about; it is one undifferentiated local store that is identical regardless of who is signed in. Partitioning Room by uid inside a Phase 1 task would be scope creep against the plan's own phase decomposition **and throwaway work**, since Room is slated for deletion in Phase 7. **Ruling: not blocking for `FB-105`.** But Phase 1's exit criterion text ("handles session changes without leaking data between users") is literally broader than what is achievable pre-Phase-2, so **`FB-106` must state explicitly** that the criterion is satisfied for auth-derived in-memory state and listeners **only**, with full data isolation deferred to Phase 2 (`FB-201`+, Firestore `users/{uid}` scoping) and Phase 7 (Room removal) — recorded, not silently assumed complete. | Orchestrator/handoff at `FB-106` |

| 2026-09-18 | DEC-005 | ACCEPTED | **The five unexercised `AuthError` paths stay a recorded bound; no live-project evidence will be gathered for them.** Scope: `UserDisabled`, `TooManyRequests`, `NetworkUnavailable`, `SessionExpired`, `Unknown` are mapped from Apple/Android SDK documentation plus pure unit tests and are **not** empirically confirmed against real SDK-emitted codes on either platform (the five that are: `EmailAlreadyInUse`, `InvalidEmail`, `WeakPassword`, `InvalidCredentials`, `UserNotFound`). Proving them would require creating, disabling, and rate-limiting real accounts in the live development project. **User decided 2026-09-18 not to do this** — the orchestrator raised it as an available option at the `FB-103` session boundary and the user chose to keep it as a recorded bound instead. Rationale: the cost (real live-project account manipulation) is not justified by the marginal confidence, and every collapse to `Unknown` carries a loud platform-side log (`FB-101-NB2`), so an unmapped real-world code degrades **visibly** rather than silently. Consequences: `FB-104`/`FB-105`/Phase 4 error-UX work must treat these five as **documentation-derived, not observed**, must not cite them as empirically proven, and must not quietly reopen this as a gate. Does **not** forbid such evidence arriving incidentally during ordinary development — it settles only that no task will be created to chase it. | User |
| 2026-09-17 | PLAN-005 | ACCEPTED | The Cloud Storage object path `users/{uid}/items/{itemId}/{photoId}` is an **interpretation**, not a plan quotation: `FIREBASE_MIGRATION_PLAN.md` line 145 says photos go under a user-owned path "**such as**" that shape, which is suggestive rather than mandatory. `FB-005` adopted it as the tighter, safer reading and the reviewer endorsed it. Recorded so the choice is auditable rather than appearing to be a literal plan requirement. | `FB-005` review |
| 2026-09-17 | PLAN-006 | ACCEPTED | Load-bearing constraint from the `FB-005` Storage Rules: the match is **exact-depth with no recursive wildcard**, so `photoRef` must be persisted as exactly `users/{uid}/items/{itemId}/{photoId}` — three fixed segments, and a `photoId` containing `/` is denied. `FB-301` (photoRef rename) and `FB-302` (PhotoStorage contract) must honor this when generating photo identifiers, and must not discover it mid-implementation. | `FB-005` review |
| 2026-09-17 | PLAN-007 | ACCEPTED | Known path asymmetry: Firestore nests items under `users/{uid}/lists/{listId}/items/{itemId}`, but Storage photos live at `users/{uid}/items/{itemId}/{photoId}` with **no `listId` segment**. Consequence: list-cascade deletion (`FB-503`) cannot sweep a Storage prefix by `listId` and must instead enumerate item documents (which do carry `listId`) to discover their `photoRef`s before deleting the list; orphan reconciliation (`FB-302`, `DEC-003`) can only key on `itemId`. Not a Rules defect — recorded now so Phase 3/5 design accounts for it. | `FB-005` review |
| 2026-09-17 | PLAN-004 | ACCEPTED | Evidence rule for all later phases: force every test task whose result is cited, then confirm the JUnit XML timestamp. `BUILD SUCCESSFUL` with an `UP-TO-DATE` test task is not evidence that tests ran. `cleanAllTests` depends only on `cleanIosSimulatorArm64Test` and does not clean either Android/JVM variant. | `FB-000` review |
| 2026-09-17 | PLAN-008 | ACCEPTED | **On iOS, Firebase-touching code must live in Swift, not in `iosMain` Kotlin.** `FB-007` selected Swift Package Manager (`firebase-ios-sdk` `12.19.2`) over the Kotlin CocoaPods integration. The plan's CocoaPods preference is conditional ("**if** it provides the cleanest supported interop"); the reviewer ruled selection of the interop mechanism to be within `FB-007`'s delegated scope ("Select and implement Apple SDK interop"), so this is **not** an unapproved architecture deviation. **Scope of the technical finding, stated precisely:** the reviewer independently confirmed from the resolved package sources that the `FirebaseStorage` SPM target is pure Swift with no public headers, and that the `FirebaseAuth` SPM target explicitly `exclude:`s its `ObjC`/`Public` directories — so via **this** dependency graph, Kotlin/Native cinterop (which consumes only ObjC/C headers) cannot reach a usable Auth/Storage surface. The **broader** claim that no pod wiring could ever work is **partially confirmed only**: `FirebaseAuth.podspec` does bundle public ObjC headers and a deprecated-symbol shim, and no end-to-end CocoaPods+cinterop build was attempted, so the blanket "impossible" framing is not fully proven (see `FB-007-NB2`). **Consequence binding Phase 2 and `FB-008`:** the plan's "temporary platform adapters in `androidMain` and `iosMain`" is satisfiable as written on Android but **not** on iOS. iOS Firebase adapters must be implemented in Swift and bridged back across the framework boundary (e.g. an `expect`/`actual` whose iOS `actual` delegates to Swift), as `FB-007` did for initialization. `FB-008`'s iOS smoke harness and `FB-201`/`FB-301` must be scoped on this basis and must not assume Kotlin-side Firebase access on iOS. | `FB-007` review + orchestrator |
| 2026-09-17 | DEC-003d | ACCEPTED | **Edit conflicts resolve by field-level last-write-wins.** Writes must patch only the fields that actually changed (`update` with a field mask / merge semantics), never replace the whole document, so concurrent edits to *different* fields on the same list or item merge automatically; only a same-field collision resolves to the later writer, silently. Rationale: the user chose this over whole-document LWW (which silently discards the other device's unrelated field changes) and over optimistic concurrency with retry (strongest correctness, but requires conflict-handling UI and error states in every edit path across Phases 2-4 — disproportionate for a single user editing their own lists across their own devices, where true same-field conflicts are rare). Consequences: `FB-201` must define DTO/field mappings so partial updates are expressible and must test that a same-field collision resolves to the later write; `FB-202`-`FB-205` must implement all mutations as field-scoped updates rather than whole-document sets; item counters remain **atomic increments** (`FieldValue.increment`) and are explicitly out of scope for LWW, since increments commute and must not be modeled as a contested field (`FB-204`); `FB-206`'s conflict tests must assert the merge behavior, not just last-writer-wins. **Cross-reference added after `FB-003` review (`FB-003-NB1`):** this interacts with `PLAN-008` — because iOS Firebase access is Swift-side only, the Swift bridge must expose an API surface able to express *which* fields changed (a field mask or changed-keys dictionary), not merely whole-document set operations. `FB-203`/`FB-205` must design that bridging surface up front rather than discovering it mid-implementation, for the same reason `PLAN-006` was recorded ahead of `FB-301`/`FB-302`. Accepted risk: a same-field conflict loses the earlier value with no user-visible notice — and per `FB-003-NB2` this is recorded as a **deliberate "no UI signal" choice**, not an oversight: `FB-401`'s error taxonomy and `FB-404`'s UI states are **not** required to surface a lost-write notification. If Phase 4 later wants one, that is a new decision, not an implementation detail. | User |
| 2026-09-22 | DEC-003d-1 | ACCEPTED | **Clarifying addendum to `DEC-003d`, not a new policy: creating a brand-new document via a fresh, collision-free auto-generated ID is exempt from the field-scoped-`FieldPatch`-only requirement.** `DEC-003d`'s text and rationale are about resolving *concurrent edits to fields on a document that already exists* ("concurrent edits to different fields on the same list or item merge automatically; only a same-field collision resolves to the later writer"). A document created via Firestore's no-argument `.document()` auto-ID has no prior state and no other client can be concurrently mutating a document that does not yet exist, so a `.set()` of its full initial field set is not the whole-document-replace-that-could-clobber-a-concurrent-edit hazard `DEC-003d` was written to prevent — it is equivalent to an atomic create. `FB-201`'s `FieldPatch` type is purpose-built for update-style patches to existing documents (its constructor cannot express document-creation semantics), so routing creation through it would be a type misuse, not a stricter reading. **Raised by:** `FB-202` reviewer round 1 (2026-09-22), ruling on the `AndroidFirebaseListRepository.createList` implementation. Recorded by the orchestrator as a decision-log addendum, not sent back to the user, because it resolves an implementation-reading ambiguity consistent with the original user-approved rationale rather than changing any user-facing tradeoff. **Consequence:** `FB-203`-`FB-205` (iOS list repository, Android/iOS item repositories) may use the same pattern — a `.set()` of the full initial field set on a fresh auto-generated document ID only, at creation time only — without re-litigating this per task. Every other mutation path (update, soft delete, restore, counters) remains field-scoped `FieldPatch`-only per `DEC-003d`, unchanged. | Orchestrator (reviewer-verified reading of already-resolved `DEC-003d`) |
| 2026-09-17 | DEC-003e | ACCEPTED | **Orphaned photos are reconciled by a server-side sweep with a grace period.** The scheduled Cloud Functions job already committed to in `DEC-003c` additionally enumerates each user's Storage objects, compares them against the `photoRef` values held by that user's item documents, and deletes unreferenced objects **older than a grace period** (originally 24h; **superseded by `DEC-003e-2`, now 30 days**). Rationale: the user chose this over client-best-effort-only (which leaks an orphan on every failure path — upload-succeeded-but-document-write-failed, mid-replace termination, offline delete — with nothing ever reclaiming them) and over explicitly accepting leakage (unbounded, billed Storage growth). Reuses infrastructure `DEC-003c` already requires, so it adds no new billing prerequisite beyond the Blaze plan. Consequences: **the grace period is load-bearing** — without it the sweep races in-flight uploads whose owning document has not yet been written, so `FB-502`/`FB-503` must treat it as a correctness requirement and test the boundary, not an optimization; per `PLAN-007` the sweep can only key on `itemId` because Storage photo paths carry no `listId` segment; per `PLAN-006` `photoRef` is exactly `users/{uid}/items/{itemId}/{photoId}`, which is what makes the compare tractable; the sweep must be idempotent and must re-check eligibility at delete time to avoid racing a restore (`FB-502`). | User |
| 2026-09-17 | DEC-003e-2 | ACCEPTED | **The orphan-sweep grace period is 30 days, not 24 hours — superseding that part of `DEC-003e`.** Raised in response to blocking review finding `FB-003-B1`, which showed the 24h window could delete a Storage object whose referencing document was still sitting in a client's offline write queue, leaving a permanently broken `photoRef` with no cleanup path back. Rationale: the user chose alignment with `DEC-003b`'s 30-day tombstone retention, which was itself sized to tolerate delayed clients, over (a) a pending-upload marker document — which closes the hole at any offline duration but costs an extra write per upload, a new document type, and its own marker-orphan cleanup — and over (b) accepting the risk. No new mechanism is introduced; one constant changes. Consequences: `FB-502`/`FB-503` must implement **30 days** as the orphan-eligibility boundary and test that boundary, and the sweep and the tombstone purge now share a single retention constant that must not drift apart; **`FB-502` design note (round-2 review):** the sweep's "referenced" set must enumerate item documents **regardless of tombstone flag** — a soft-deleted item's `photoRef` counts as still referenced for the full tombstone window. If tombstoned documents were excluded from the referenced-set query, a photo could become orphan-eligible on a different clock than its item's 30-day restore window, and a naive implementation could delete a photo the user can still restore. This is distinct from, and additional to, the existing re-check-at-delete-time requirement; genuinely abandoned uploads occupy Storage for 30 days before reclamation, which is accepted. **Residual risk, explicitly accepted:** a client offline for more than 30 days between uploading bytes and its document write landing can still produce a broken reference — the same bound that already applies to tombstone recovery, so the system now has one coherent offline-durability horizon rather than two. | User |
| 2026-09-17 | DEC-004 | ACCEPTED | **`MAN-003` (iOS physical-device signing/access) is permanently `WAIVED`; the iOS target for this project is the simulator.** Rationale given by the user: this is not an App Store-bound project and there is no plan to distribute it or run it on physical iOS hardware, so device signing is not a deferred obligation but a requirement that does not apply. This is a **deliberate scope decision, not an oversight or an unmet gate** — a future session must not reopen it as a blocker. Verified before accepting: the plan's Phase 0 exit criterion requires only that "Android and iOS can authenticate and complete the Firestore/Storage smoke test" (no device clause); the Definition of Done contains no physical-device requirement; and the app's photo flow uses `PHPickerViewController` (photo library), **not** camera capture, which the simulator supports — had it used the camera, this waiver would not have been viable. **Plan line explicitly overridden (not glossed):** the Phase 0 integration-spike list in `FIREBASE_MIGRATION_PLAN.md:76` names "iOS simulator **and physical-device** builds" as a spike target. This waiver **supersedes that line for this project**, on the grounds that it is a spike checklist item rather than a phase exit criterion or a Definition-of-Done clause (neither of which mentions a device), and that the reason for the line — confidence that the app runs on real hardware — does not apply to a project that will not run on real hardware. Recording this explicitly so the decision is not later read as having missed the conflict. Consequences: (1) `FB-011` is satisfied by the waiver plus `FB-007`'s simulator linking/init evidence; (2) `FB-008`'s "two-platform" smoke matrix means Android + **iOS simulator**; (3) `FB-706`'s final matrix is amended from "iOS device" to "iOS simulator"; (4) `FB-405`'s airplane-mode checks are performed by cutting host networking, which approximates but does not reproduce true device airplane mode — recorded as a known fidelity limit, not a pass/fail gap. **Permanently unverified and accepted as such:** code signing, provisioning, embed-and-sign, on-device Keychain token persistence, real device memory/performance behavior, and **real-device background-suspension/termination behavior under memory pressure** (relevant to `FB-405`'s background/foreground and process-restart leg, which is therefore exercised at simulator fidelity only). If this project ever becomes App Store-bound, this decision must be explicitly reopened — the first real-device build would exercise all of the above for the first time. **Open question deliberately NOT decided here:** whether `MAN-005`/`FB-707` production provisioning still applies at all given the project is not shipping; `DEC-002a` already defers production topology to Phase 7, and this is a separate decision for the user when Phase 7 is reached. | User |
| 2026-09-24 | DEC-007 | ACCEPTED | **`FB-404`'s cache/pending-writes state machine is accepted as delivered against its own literal acceptance criterion (unit-test-only), but a new task `FB-407` is added — a hard dependency of `FB-405` and `FB-406`, not a soft non-blocking follow-up — to wire the real Android/iOS Firestore adapters before Phase 4 can close.** Raised by the `FB-404` reviewer: `Repositories.kt`'s new `observeListSummariesSnapshot()`/`observeItemsSnapshot()` default to reporting `isFromCache=false`/`hasPendingWrites=false` unconditionally, and none of the four real adapters (`AndroidFirebaseListRepository`/`AndroidFirebaseItemRepository`/`IosFirestoreListBridge`/`IosFirestoreItemBridge`) override that default — so `FB-404`'s `Loaded(isFromCache=true, ...)`/`Loaded(hasPendingWrites=true, ...)` branch is genuinely unreachable in production, not merely under-tested. Rationale for treating this as a required task rather than a soft follow-up: Phase 4's plan exit criterion is "airplane-mode and interrupted-upload testing produces understandable, recoverable behavior," and `FB-405`'s manual matrix (as originally scoped, depending only on `FB-404`) could be run and reported "PASS" without ever exercising this behavior, since no real signal exists yet to observe — `FB-406` could then close a gate whose exit criterion was never actually demonstrated. This is a technical sequencing/scope correction, not a new product tradeoff, so it is recorded by the orchestrator without returning to the user, consistent with the `DEC-003d-1` precedent (resolves an implementation-completeness gap rather than changing a user-facing decision already made). **Consequence:** `FB-407` is appended at the end of the ledger with a fresh, never-reused ID per the workflow's stable-ID rule, even though it is a hard dependency of the numerically-earlier `FB-405`/`FB-406` — task IDs reflect insertion order, not execution order. `FB-405`'s `Depends on` widened from `FB-404` to `FB-404, FB-407`; `FB-406`'s widened from `FB-405` to `FB-405, FB-407` for defense-in-depth at the gate audit. The P4 phase-gate row's Required tasks list now reads `FB-401…FB-406, FB-407`. | Orchestrator (reviewer-driven technical finding, not a user product decision) |
| 2026-09-18 | DEC-006 | ACCEPTED | **The session gate times out a hung initial `restoreSession()` after 10 seconds and falls back to the signed-out auth screen with a non-alarming 'could not restore your session' notice plus a retry affordance.** Resolves `FB-104-NB2`, which the `FB-104` reviewer deferred to `FB-105` on the grounds that the duration and fallback UX are a product decision rather than a correctness fix. Rationale given by the user: a bounded spinner that always degrades to a resolved, signed-out state is preferable to an indefinite one, it never displays user data without a resolved UID, and it is consistent with `FB-102-NB2`'s requirement for a 'sign out and retry' recovery affordance rather than retry-only. Consequence: `FB-105` must implement the timeout in `SessionGateViewModel` and cover it with an automated test driving a fake whose `restoreSession()` never completes; the 10s value is a product constant, not a correctness threshold, and may be retuned later without reopening this decision. Rejected alternatives: a 30s window (longer worst-case spinner), a dedicated timeout **error** screen (diverges from `DEC-003a`/`FB-404`'s loading-not-error framing), and deferring again (would leave `FB-104-NB2` open past the phase it was assigned to). | User |
| 2026-09-25 | DEC-008 | ACCEPTED | **A new task `FB-408` is added — a hard dependency of `FB-406`, not a soft non-blocking follow-up — to fix an uncaught-terminal-listener-error crash discovered while running `FB-405`'s matrix, on both `DashboardViewModel` and `ListDetailViewModel`.** Raised by the `FB-405` developer and independently confirmed by the `FB-405` reviewer via direct source reading (not merely trusted): `DashboardViewModel.kt`'s and `ListDetailViewModel.kt`'s `combine(...).stateIn(viewModelScope, ...)` chains have no `.catch` anywhere, and both `AndroidFirebaseListRepository`/`IosFirebaseListRepository`'s `observeListSummariesSnapshot()`/equivalent call `close(exception)` on a terminal listener error (e.g. a real `PERMISSION_DENIED` once the backing auth token is invalidated — sign-out, a revoked/expired token, or a stale persisted session found invalid on cold launch), which `callbackFlow`'s contract rethrows to every downstream collector; a repo-wide grep confirms no `CoroutineExceptionHandler` exists anywhere to catch it before it reaches `viewModelScope`'s `Dispatchers.Main.immediate` and crashes the app process. The reviewer independently found the identical unguarded pattern in `ListDetailViewModel.kt` (not disclosed by the developer, who scoped the finding to `DashboardViewModel` only) via direct source reading, and recommends `ItemDetailViewModel` be re-audited before the new task is called complete. **Rationale for treating this as a required task rather than a soft follow-up:** Phase 4's plan exit criterion is "understandable, recoverable behavior rather than indefinite spinners or silent failure" — a process crash is strictly worse than either, so `FB-406` cannot honestly close the phase gate while this stands. This is distinct from the already-`OPEN` `FB-402-NB1`/`FB-403-NB2` (a mis-mapped-as-retryable UX quality gap): the crash is an availability defect upstream of any mutation-level try/catch, and fixing `FB-402-NB1`/`FB-403-NB2` would not close it. This is a technical sequencing/scope correction, not a new product tradeoff, so it is recorded by the orchestrator without returning to the user, consistent with the `DEC-003d-1`/`DEC-007` precedent. **Consequence:** `FB-408` is appended at the end of the ledger with a fresh, never-reused ID per the workflow's stable-ID rule. `FB-406`'s `Depends on` widened from `FB-405, FB-407` to `FB-405, FB-407, FB-408`. The P4 phase-gate row's Required tasks list now reads `FB-401…FB-406, FB-407, FB-408`. `FB-405` itself is not blocked by this and proceeds to `VERIFIED` — its own scope explicitly excluded fixing `FB-404`/`FB-407` product code, only observing and evidencing behavior, which the developer correctly did (flagging the defect rather than silently expanding scope). | Orchestrator (reviewer-driven technical finding, not a user product decision) |
| 2026-09-25 | DEC-009 | ACCEPTED | **A new task `FB-409` is added — a hard dependency of `FB-406`, not a soft non-blocking follow-up — to fix an uncaught-exception crash in `ListDetailViewModel.deleteList()`, discovered by the `FB-406` gate reviewer.** `deleteList()` is a bare `viewModelScope.launch { listRepository.softDeleteList(listId); listDeleted.value = true }` with no `try/catch/finally`, no duplicate-submit guard, and zero test coverage; a real Firestore failure on this mutation (e.g. `PERMISSION_DENIED` after session invalidation) rethrows as `ListRepositoryException` uncaught through `viewModelScope`, crashing the app process — structurally the identical crash mechanism `DEC-008`/`FB-408` eliminated for listener-observation chains, just on a mutation path instead. This gap was previously disclosed as `FB-403-NB1` and ruled "a deliberate, sound scope boundary... not an oversight," which was correct **at the time** (`FB-403` was scoped to item-level operations only) but that ruling predates `FB-405`'s discovery of the crash class and `DEC-008`'s creation specifically to close it — `FB-406`'s own gate-audit claim that no crash/silent-failure path remained open was therefore overstated and is corrected by this decision, not left standing. **Rationale for treating this as a required task rather than re-litigating `FB-403-NB1`'s disposition or silently reopening `FB-403`:** Phase 4's plan exit criterion is "understandable, recoverable behavior... not... silent failure" — a process crash violates this more severely than either a spinner or a silent failure, so `FB-406` cannot honestly close the phase gate while a known instance of this exact crash class stands, regardless of which task's scope it falls in. This is a technical sequencing/scope correction, not a new product tradeoff, so it is recorded by the orchestrator without returning to the user, consistent with the `DEC-003d-1`/`DEC-007`/`DEC-008` precedent. **Consequence:** `FB-409` is appended at the end of the ledger with a fresh, never-reused ID per the workflow's stable-ID rule, dependency `FB-408` (`DONE`, so `FB-409` is immediately selectable). `FB-406`'s `Depends on` widened from `FB-405, FB-407, FB-408` to `FB-405, FB-407, FB-408, FB-409`; the P4 phase-gate row's Required tasks list now reads `FB-401…FB-406, FB-407, FB-408, FB-409`. `FB-409` must also re-audit every other ViewModel mutation method for the same bare-`launch`-with-no-guard shape, since `FB-406-B1` was found by re-reading source rather than an exhaustive sweep and a sibling gap of the same shape must not be assumed absent. | Orchestrator (reviewer-driven technical finding, not a user product decision) |
